AI-First or AI-Invisible? The Boating Industry's AI Reality Check

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

New Similarweb data shows Boat Trader and YachtWorld generating more than 17 times the AI-referred traffic of the next-closest competing marketplace, highlighting the growing importance of trust, scale, and credible data in AI-powered discovery.

MIAMI, Nov. 11, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- As artificial intelligence reshapes how consumers discover and buy boats, new third-party data from Similarweb shows Boat Trader and YachtWorld leading the marine industry in traffic from AI-powered search.

From July through September 2026, the two Boats Group marketplaces generated more than 17 times the estimated monthly AI-referred traffic of the next-closest competing marine marketplace in the United States, highlighting the growing importance of trusted brands, comprehensive data, and consumer reach in AI-powered discovery.

Who is actually getting traffic from AI?Average monthly U.S. AI-referred visits â¢ JulyâSeptember 2026

As consumers turn to platforms like ChatGPT and Google's AI-powered search experiences, the way buyers discover boats is changing. Rather than searching multiple websites, a buyer can ask, "What's the best center console for fishing with a family of six?" or "Which cruising yacht offers the best value under $500,000?"

AI tools can help consumers research options, compare models, and narrow their choices before they ever visit a marketplace or dealership. AI-powered discovery depends on relevant, trustworthy, and accessible information. Comprehensive inventory, accurate specifications, original reviews, useful buying guidance, and regularly updated data all help position marketplaces for discovery.

Boat Trader, YachtWorld, and boats.com have spent decades establishing themselves as trusted destinations for boat buyers. Today, these marketplaces attract approximately 16 million monthly visitors, supported by extensive inventory, manufacturer information, buyer insights, and industry expertise.

Trust attracts traffic. Sustained engagement builds digital presence. And credible, comprehensive information creates more opportunities for AI-powered discovery.

"AI is creating an entirely new opportunity to connect buyers with the right boats, and we're seeing that reflected in measurable consumer activity," said John Barrile, Chief Technology Officer at Boats Group. "Our longstanding investment in trusted marketplaces, comprehensive data, and technology has positioned Boats Group to play a leading role in this next generation of search. We're focused on extending that advantage to the dealers, brokers, and manufacturers who rely on our platforms to reach buyers."

As AI becomes a more influential part of the buying journey, marketplace visibility is expanding beyond traditional search engines and websites. For dealers and brokers, this creates additional opportunities for inventory to reach consumers as they research, compare, and narrow their choices.

For manufacturers, AI-powered recommendations can influence which brands and models consumers consider before visiting a dealership. Boats Group's buyer research found brand loyalty rates as low as 6â13% across the segments analyzed, underscoring the importance of building brand preference early in the buying journey.

For industry partners, this creates a new opportunity to reach buyers earlier in their search, making visibility across AI-powered platforms an increasingly important part of marketplace performance.

Boats Group is extending its reach through direct integrations with ChatGPT and Meta's AI ecosystem, making customer inventory, manufacturer information, and industry expertise discoverable beyond its own marketplaces. The goal is simple: help dealers, brokers, and manufacturers reach buyers wherever they choose to search.

While AI-referred traffic doesn't capture every instance in which a marketplace appears in an AI-generated answer, it provides a measurable indication of whether consumers are moving from AI platforms to marketplace websites.

The bottom line: As more buyers turn to AI to find their next boat, being visible where they search matters more than ever.

Boats Group operates the world's leading online marketplaces for buying and selling boats, including Boat Trader, YachtWorld, and boats.com. With a global audience of millions of boat buyers, Boats Group provides data-driven marketing solutions, technology, financing services, and industry insights that help OEMs, dealers, and brokers connect with consumers throughout the boat-buying journey.

The Lenses of Truth allow you to see through illusions in OoT, at the cost of magic power

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Lenses of Truth in Zelda Ocarina of Time are an essential item used to reveal hidden objects, illusions, and secrets that Link might have otherwise missed. In the original OoT these were actually called the Lens of Truth, presented as a flamboyant magnifying glass, but in the Remake they're recreated into a pair of equally flashy glasses. Either way, the effect and their location in Kakariko Village remains the same, so I'll explain where to find them in the Remake below.

To get the Lenses of Truth in OoT, you need to have progressed to the point where you can travel between eras in the Temple of Time. Once you've done that, however, you can obtain the Lenses with the followng steps:

Lenses of Truth can be equipped like any of the masks from the Happy Mask Shop. However, while being worn, they drain your magic constantly.

The benefit is that while wearing them, you can see through illusions and other optical effects. These include:

Apple's touchscreen MacBook Pro does not like to be taken apart

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Easy lid swaps earn praise from iFixit, but panel removal ends in cracks and USB-C repairs require logic board surgery

Apple's first touchscreen MacBook got a mixed reception from iFixit's teardown specialists, who praised its easily replaced lid but found fans and USB-C ports buried beneath the logic board.

The MacBook Pro with PROLED XDR display was unveiled at the end of October. It features a tandem OLED screen that stacks two layers of organic light-emitting pixels, eliminating the need for a backlight, and adds touch capability to the MacBook line for the first time.

Although the vendor was patted on the back in recent years for improving the repairability of its famously locked-down products, this machine shows Apple still has some way to go.

The good? The entire lid comes off relatively easily. Its three-screw hinges closely resemble those on the M6 14-inch MacBook Pro, while streamlined display cable brackets simplify removal.

Apple's Repair Assistant tool also handled a full system board swap well. Despite concerns about Touch ID fingerprint authentication, everything went relatively smoothly when iFixit paired a touchscreen from one machine with a logic board from another. On the first boot, Repair Assistant recognized the battery, display, and Touch ID sensor, and touch control needed no separate calibration.

The bad? Replacing the panel alone is not possible. When iFixit tried to separate it from the lid, the glass delaminated, cracking the OLED and touch-sensitive layers beneath. That could make for a costlier repair than bargained for.

Under the bottom cover, the PROLED has a similar internal layout to the last few models of MacBook Pros, with the fans, board, speakers, and trackpad all in the same places.

That means the same annoyances persist. The MagSafe power connector can be removed via two screws and a bracket, yet the USB-C ports can't be extracted the same way because the logic board is in the way. The headphone jack and the fans also require removing the system board first, and the HDMI port and SDXC card reader are soldered to the board itself, as is the storage and memory.

Another gripe is that this MacBook's battery pack is hard to remove, though at least it is removable, unlike in many thin-and-light laptops. The tested Pro weighs 1.38 kg or about 3 pounds.

For these reasons, the MacBook Pro with PROLED XDR display earns a 5 out of 10 repairability score from iFixit, but this is actually a one point increase over the score it gave the M5 MacBook Pro.

The Apple logo has its own lighting. Older MacBooks with illuminated logos borrowed light from the LCD backlight shining through an apple-shaped window in the lid. This model uses a strip of LEDs along one edge of the logo, passing through a diffuser for an even glow. Because it sits behind the display panel, it would be nearly impossible to repair if it failed, especially if the panel is destroyed during removal.

Under a microscope, the PROLED XDR display looks very different from earlier MacBooks: the blue subpixel is significantly larger than the green and red ones.

The reason, we're told, is that blue OLED materials wear out faster, so a larger subpixel can emit the same amount of light while putting less strain on the material. ®

Patrick Stewart’s Favorite Star Trek Episode Is Popular With Fans, But He Loved It For A Different Reason

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Read reviews, interviews and deep dives on new releases.

Get the latest movie, TV and streaming news delivered to your inbox.

Star Trek's John De Lancie's Tribute To Patrick Stewart Dropped The Perfect Q Reference I Can't Get Over

I Didn’t See It Coming When William Shatner Made A Morbid Joke After Losing Two Star Trek Co-Stars Just Days Apart

Hugh Jackman, William Shatner, And More Send Touching Tributes After Sir Patrick Stewart’s Death

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I think Patrick Stewart gave many great performances on Star Trek: The Next Generation, but I've always wondered which episode stood out as his favorite. I finally found out more after the actor's passing, and while I wasn't surprised by his choice, I didn't see it coming when his reason was way different from the fans.

If you're a Trek fan, you probably know "The Inner Light" is generally considered one of the best episodes of The Next Generation. I wouldn't have been surprised if Stewart loved the episode because it put Picard in a completely different setting than he was used to, and while that's (probably) part of it, he told Wired there was another reason it actually stood out.

"The Inner Light" was a unique episode for Patrick Stewart because it allowed him to spread his wings and do something other than the traditional performance of Jean-Luc Picard. As he mentioned when asked about it, he was equally thrilled that he got to work alongside a beloved family member.

Because I become someone other than Jean-Luc Picard over decades of living a different life, and therefore become a different person, a domestic person, not a starship captain. And there is another, personal reason. My son Daniel played my son in ‘The Inner Light.’ That was an extraordinary experience.

It's great to hear that Patrick Stewart loved working with his son so much, though it's certainly bittersweet. It's known that Stewart had an estranged relationship with his children, as People reported that their relationship with him was practically nonexistent when they were small, and things didn't improve later on.

Stewart wrote in later years that he regretted putting his career before his own children. In this instance, he was able to blend both into one, and it makes the story more than a little heartbreaking to read. It certainly will change the way I see the episode the next time I see it with a Paramount+ subscription.

For those unfamiliar with the episode, "The Inner Light" revolves around Picard after he is struck with a mysterious beam that renders him unconscious. While he's out, he proceeds to live an alternate life as Kamin, a scientist on a faraway planet with a wife and family. He lives for decades on the planet, only to awaken later as Picard. The bigger twist? Only twenty-five minutes have passed.

Ian McKellen's Comments About Late Friend Patrick Stewart Are Making Me Emotional

It's interesting in hindsight to hear Patrick Stewart talk about this episode and see the parallels in his own life. Keep in mind, the actor was working pretty much up until his death, even hinting months prior he'd never retire. Perhaps one of the reasons Picard was one of Star Trek's best characters was because he was played by someone who understood the concept of being married to a career, and the pain that comes along with it.

All this to say, it's clear Stewart was beloved by all he worked with, and I haven't read a negative thing about him. He did more in his time on Earth than some could do in two lifetimes, so I'm not surprised to hear that, in his old age, he wished he could've done more.

Watch Picard in Star Trek: The Next Generation, which is available to stream right now on Paramount+. I may have to check out "The Inner Light" today after learning about this, though I think it'll be more emotional for me to watch now than it was back then.

Mick Joest is a Content Producer for CinemaBlend with his hand in an eclectic mix of television goodness. Star Trek is his main jam, but he also regularly reports on happenings in the world of Star Trek, WWE, Doctor Who, 90 Day Fiancé, Quantum Leap, and Big Brother. He graduated from the University of Southern Indiana with a degree in Journalism and a minor in Radio and Television. He's great at hosting panels and appearing on podcasts if given the chance as well.

Former Southampton and Manchester United midfielder Morgan Schneiderlin explains why he thinks Mauricio Pochettino will be a success at Old Trafford

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Manchester United look set to change manager following a run of just one win in 10 league matches under Michael Carrick.

Mauricio Pochettino is in the picture to replace Carrick, meanwhile former Old Trafford and Southampton midfielder Morgan Schneiderlin has delivered an emphatic endorsement of the Argentine coach.

Pochettino is widely considered the leading candidate to take charge, and Schneiderlin - who played the finest football of his career under the Argentine at St Mary’s Stadium - insists the 54-year-old has the personal qualities required to unify a fractured club.

Reflecting on Pochettino’s arrival in English football in 2013, Schneiderlin revealed that an initial language barrier did nothing to dilute the manager’s authority.

"He didn't need to speak a lot of the language," Schneiderlin recalls, via Comeon.com. "He would put an arm around you, and with the translator or someone who spoke English, he’d explain what he liked about you and exactly what he wanted you to do.

"I'll always remember his team talk before that first game against Everton. He was just screaming in Spanish, a deeply motivational speech, entirely in Spanish, but all we could hear and understand was: 'Press like lions! Press like lions! Press like lions!' And we did press like lions!

"Now that he speaks fluent English, it's going to be even easier for him. But his charisma, his presence, and the way he carried himself - we were just under his spell straight away."

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

That buy-in was cemented on the Saints' training ground, where Pochettino instituted a relentless physical culture.

"We were working very, very hard in training sessions and in the gym, not to be bodybuilders or look nice on the beach, but building a lot of lower-body strength," Schneiderlin explains.

"He used me much more as a true box-to-box midfielder - he wanted me to run, to press, and to win the ball back. It was very enjoyable."

However, Schneiderlin has warned against expecting Pochettino to instantly rely on academy graduates to dig Man United out of trouble, stressing that Old Trafford 'demands results immediately' and requires a stable footing first.

Having since managed Paris Saint-Germain and Chelsea, Pochettino will set foot inside Old Trafford a more battle-hardened version of the coach who took charge at Southampton 13 years ago.

"He arrives now with so much experience behind him and so much baggage, both good and bad, he has learned from," Schneiderlin added. "He will use everything he learned from being in Paris Saint-Germain dealing with big players and big personalities."

Overwatch is adding 6v6 role queue to its Competitive play: 'We've realised that there doesn't have to be a winner between 5v5 and 6v6'

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

This town is big enough for the both of us.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If you had told me back in 2019 that I'd actually prefer a 5v5 version of Overwatch instead of the traditional 6v6 in the big old year of 2026 I'd have replied: "I'm still playing Overwatch in 2026?" Regardless, it's a strange revelation for sure (the 5v5 preference, not that I'm still playing Overwatch), but as time has gone on more players have discovered a similar love for the slightly more simplistic mode.

While I prefer 5v5 for its more focused fights, emphasis on individual plays, and overall less cluttered nonsense I do also still enjoy playing a game of 6v6 every now and then. The only issue is that for the longest time the only version available in quickplay and competitive was the 6v6 open queue. This option may offer faster queue times, but with that often brings absolutely zero team cohesion.

But teams of four supports and two DPS may be a thing of the past now that Team 4 is trialling a 6v6 role queue for competitive. "There will be three Competitive options of the game available on Day 1 of Season 6: our standard 5v5, Open Queue 6v6, and our Role Queue 6v6 test," game director Aaron Keller says in a blog post. "The 2-2-2 Role Queue Competitive mini season will start at season launch and run for the first four weeks of the season. We’ll also keep the Open Queue version of 6v6 running and the balance patch will be applied to that mode as well."

"There are also some matchmaking changes shipping with Season 6 that we theorise will keep queue times more manageable."

Team 4 is also keeping the 6v6 open queue around for scientific purposes, as the devs will use it to help determine whether players queue 6v6 because they love the game mode, or because they love the shorter queue times. The team has already acknowledged that queue times for 6v6 role queue may be a bit lengthy as it'll have to find not one, but two whole tanks per team for a game to start. Although Keller does add that "there are also some matchmaking changes shipping with Season 6 that we theorise will keep queue times more manageable."

Do I think this matchmaking change will solve queue times for 6v6 role queue? Probably not. But if it helps ease it, even slightly, then we may have a winner between 5v5 and 6v6. As many players switched over to the former due to the lack of tanks (and therefore long wait times) for the latter.

Keller has stressed that this popularity contest isn't about ousting one game mode: "Over the past few years, we’ve realised that there doesn’t have to be a 'winner' between 5v5 and 6v6. The real competition should be in your matches, not between two modes that can coexist. You can play either one (many people actually play both!) and we’re going to support that choice.

"Currently, we don’t have any intention of moving the main mode of Overwatch to 6v6, but we’re starting to think of it as a second among equals. We’ll put more energy into balance patches for the mode and we’ll continue to look for the right format that really gives our community the experience they’re looking for."

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

Team 4's approach to Overwatch's different game modes is a great example of how to handle a live service game. Instead of forcing a specific game mode onto players, letting the community decide what works best for them by voting with their playtime ensures that players are getting what they want. The only issue I've seen with it is that it whittles out smaller more experimental modes like Stadium, as the playerbase is split between competitive and quickplay and then two, going on three core modes.

I liked Stadium, and was sad to see it go. But as I said before, players vote with their time in Overwatch, and with only 6% regularly choosing Stadium it was clearly not a smart investment for Team 4 to continue putting so much work into it.

Team 4's decision to slowly experiment by bringing back old modes instead of shoehorning them into the game is smart, and will likely make for a better experience in the long run, regardless of if the 6v6 role queue makes it past the first four weeks of Season 6 or not.

"One of the things that I hope you take away from this is that we’re always trying to improve Overwatch in ways that matter to you," Keller adds. "Just don’t start asking for 7v7!" Huh, now you've said it 7v7 could be quite fun.

Why Sandisk Stock Popped Today

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CoreWeave's good earnings report is great news for Sandisk stock.

A strong earnings report from AI cloud provider CoreWeave (CRWV +21.77%) last night is lifting shares of AI data center memory chip provider Sandisk (SNDK +14.12%) this morning.

As of 10:10 a.m. ET, CoreWeave stock is up 20.3% -- and Sandisk is up 13%.

CoreWeave beat earnings in its Q3 report last night, losing less money ($1.27 per share) than feared, and collecting more revenue ($3.66 billion) than hoped. That company further raised its guidance for full-year 2026 revenue to a range of from $13.0 billion to $13.3 billion, with better-than-forecast adjusted operating income guidance as well -- and promising to spend anywhere from $37 billion to $39 billion on capital investment.

That's the part most relevant to investors' hopes for Sandisk. The more money CoreWeave spends on "capital investment," the more money it will be sending Sandisk's way for computer memory chips to outfit its data centers. This is a bullish forecast for Sandisk's revenue as well.

Just as important is CoreWeave's ability to pay for all these chips, and CoreWeave had more good news on that front. Not only will 2026 revenue be above expectations, but CoreWeave raised its prediction for the revenue it will have coming in on an annual run rate basis -- to $20 billion to $21 billion as it exits 2026. And CoreWeave predicted it will be earning at least "adjusted" profits by early 2028.

CoreWeave's good news means the future is looking brighter for Sandisk stock, too. At less than 7x trailing earnings and an ultra-cheap less than 4x forward earnings, now might be a good time to buy Sandisk stock.

Prediction: After Losing Over $1.7 Trillion in Market Cap This Year, Nvidia Stock Will Rebound in Epic Fashion. Here's Why.

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia stock has crashed 27% over the last month.

For much of 2026, Nvidia (NVDA +2.44%) looked unstoppable. The artificial intelligence (AI) chipmaker delivered strong financial results, while Wall Street rewarded its dominance with a lofty valuation. In early October, Nvidia's market capitalization peaked around $5.8 trillion, making it the world's most valuable company.

Unfortunately, the momentum didn't last. As of this writing (Nov. 10), Nvidia's market cap has fallen to $4.1 trillion. That's a decline of 29%, representing roughly $1.7 trillion in lost value.

The sell-off reflects growing uncertainty surrounding AI infrastructure spending, political developments, and a new investigation involving CEO Jensen Huang. Nevertheless, I think investors are overlooking something important.

Nvidia's stock price has changed dramatically, but its underlying competitive advantages haven't disappeared. Here's why I think the semiconductor behemoth could stage a comeback in 2027.

Nvidia's meteoric rise isn't simply the result of investors being excited about AI. It was actually supported by unprecedented growth in revenue and profit. During the company's fiscal second quarter, Nvidia generated $96.2 billion in revenue, representing a 106% increase year over year. The data center business contributed $89 billion, growing 117% annually, while earnings per share (EPS) surged 128% to $2.46.

Nvidia's growth is an extension of the capital expenditure (capex) commitments from hyperscalers including Microsoft, Amazon, Alphabet, Meta Platforms, and Oracle.

As big tech expands its computing infrastructure to train and operate AI models, companies are purchasing Nvidia's GPUs, networking equipment, and integrated computing systems. The company's Blackwell architecture has been instrumental in meeting this demand, while the newer Vera Rubin platform is entering full-scale production.

Nvidia is also expanding into specialized inference accelerators and agentic AI. In other words, Nvidia isn't simply selling individual chips. It's building a comprehensive stack of hardware and software for the next generation of AI. This explains why investors were willing to assign the company such a premium valuation.

Nvidia's valuation increasingly depends on the assumption that AI infrastructure spending will continue expanding at an unprecedented pace. However, investors are beginning to question how quickly the hyperscalers can generate enough returns on their data center investments.

The midterm elections introduced another layer of uncertainty. With Democrats gaining control of Congress, investors are reassessing the possibility of additional scrutiny surrounding data center construction, energy consumption, and electricity prices. While this doesn't mean AI spending will suddenly collapse, tighter regulations or slower project approvals could affect the timing of infrastructure investments.

More recently, Nvidia has encountered an entirely different challenge. Reports concerning CEO Jensen Huang's relationship with a business executive suspected of ties to Chinese intelligence prompted Nvidia's board of directors to launch an investigation.

While the board says it has no reason to believe company secrets or national security information were compromised, the situation introduces governance and potential succession risks. If Huang is eventually replaced or steps down as CEO, investors would understandably question how the transition might affect Nvidia's strategy, customer relationships, and execution. For now, the uncertainty itself is contributing to outsize pressure on Nvidia shares.

Despite these challenges, I think Nvidia remains exceptionally well positioned for long-term growth. Consider the company's competitive position. Advanced Micro Devices is expanding its AI accelerator business, while Broadcom is helping companies develop custom chips.

However, neither competitor has replicated Nvidia's ecosystem of GPUs, networking technology, and the CUDA software system. This ecosystem matters because it makes switching suppliers considerably time-consuming and expensive, creating meaningful barriers to competition.

Even if data center construction slows, that doesn't necessarily mean Nvidia's existing customers will stop purchasing the company's products. Cloud providers and frontier labs will still need to continue upgrading infrastructure, replacing older systems, and supporting growing demand for AI inference.

Even if Nvidia eventually operates under a different CEO, the company's technology, intellectual property (IP), customer relationships, and engineering talent aren't going to vanish overnight. Likewise, slower growth in AI infrastructure spending doesn't automatically eliminate Nvidia's profitability.

Nvidia could look different a year from now. Its leadership might change, its customers could become more selective, and its growth rate might moderate. Nevertheless, I expect Nvidia to remain one of the most important and profitable companies in the AI industry.

In the short term, stock prices are often driven by sentiment, uncertainty, and headlines. Over the long term, earnings growth and valuation tend to matter far more. That's why I think Nvidia's recent sell-off could look like a compelling buying opportunity, and why I'm predicting a meaningful recovery in 2027.

Prediction: A Bear Market Is Coming: 3 Ways to Stay Ahead

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The AI-led Trump boom may be on its last legs.

The past few years have been a bonanza for stock market investors, with the benchmark S&P 500 index (^GSPC +0.80%) soaring by roughly 26% in 2023, 25% in 2024, and 18% in 2025. The final tally for 2026 remains to be seen, but it looks increasingly clear that market sentiment is cooling, and we could potentially be on track for a bear market in 2027.

The biggest challenge is uncertainty about the sustainability of generative AI-related capital spending in the economy. The changing US political climate could add new risks from more regulation on data center construction and new energy projects. Let's discuss three ways investors can protect their portfolios in this increasingly uncertain stock market.

The stock market is more concentrated than ever before, with America's top five tech giants (Apple, Nvidia, Microsoft, Amazon, and Alphabet) representing an eye-popping 30% of the entire S&P 500. This situation is dangerous because it means the index no longer offers a high level of diversification, and any decline in these stocks will have a huge impact on the index's overall return. There are big reasons to be worried.

With the exception of Apple, all five of these companies have gone all-in on AI technology, developing their own large language models (LLMs) and spending hundreds of billions to acquire the hardware needed to run and train these algorithms for major clients like OpenAI and Anthropic.

A recent study published by the WSJ suggests that the level of spending is so high that American businesses and consumers will have to spend the equivalent of 8.8% of the country's annual gross domestic product (GDP) on AI-related products and services by 2032 to make it worth it. This seems extremely unlikely, considering that it's more than energy spending and almost as much as the amount spent on food.

Furthermore, open-source models and low-cost Chinese rivals are rapidly gaining ground on their expensive American rivals, introducing the risk of margin-eroding competition that limits revenue and profit potential.

Increasing portfolio diversification is key to coming out ahead if the potential AI bubble bursts. And thankfully, there are a lot of options investors can choose from. We can look at the example of Warren Buffett for some ideas. The investing legend's Berkshire Hathaway portfolio currently holds a record 'cash' position of $365.5 billion, mostly in highly liquid securities like short-term Treasury bonds.

These assets offer a compelling risk-free yield of over 4%, which can make them a good alternative to risky stocks. Plus, they allow investors to quickly respond to opportunities in the market if stocks get cheaper over the next few months.

Investors can also look at more 'boring' and recession-resistant areas in the market like tobacco and consumer-essentials-focused real estate investment trusts (REITs), which also tend to offer good yields while being far less exposed to a potential slowdown in AI-related spending.

The stock market has a long track record of booms and busts, but over the long term it always bounces back. That's why time spent in the market tends to beat trying to time the market. The influence of government stimulus packages and the Federal Reserve's countercyclical policies (such as lowering interest rates or quantitative easing) also help to ensure that downturns are less extreme and briefer than ever before.

We can look to the example of the recent COVID-19 crash, which sent the S&P 500 down by roughly 18% in 2022, only to be followed by a rebound of 26% in 2023. I don't expect a bursting of the AI bubble to be much different. Investors should keep a long-term perspective and be ready to quickly react to any deals that show up in the coming years.

China’s Nvidia Honeypot Isn’t Surprising. Jensen Huang Fell for It Anyway

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There’s a specific mantra drilled into government workers, military officers, and intelligence personnel during counterintelligence briefings: “If she’s a 10 and you’re a 5, she’s a spy!” Or, as the US Army’s Counterintelligence Command posted on Valentine’s Day: “It doesn’t take a mathematician to figure out ‘10 + 5 = honeytrap.’” The very beautiful woman who is suddenly interested in your very ordinary existence probably has ulterior motives, is the message.

Jensen Huang surely doesn’t live a typical life—he’s a centibillionaire, the swashbuckling CEO of Nvidia, one of the hottest and most geopolitically consequential companies of the 21st century. Surely he’s aware of the possibility that he’d cross paths with intelligence assets. In fact, it’s hard to think of many companies in the world that would be a higher priority target for foreign intelligence agencies.

All of that makes the stock-shaking news this week all the more surprising. How did Huang get embroiled in a spy scandal that mixes infidelity, international espionage, the Trump administration, and the economic behemoth of the multitrillion-dollar AI industry?

The story kicked off just before Halloween, with a six-byline story in The New York Times reporting that Huang was the target of a Chinese spy plot. Starting somewhere between 2024 and 2025, Huang allegedly had an affair with Alice Huang (no relation), a one-time executive at a Singapore-based cloud provider known as Megaspeed. US intelligence agencies warned top US officials that they believed she was a Chinese intelligence asset. So far, Nvidia’s board, which has launched its own investigation, says it has “no reason to believe that either national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised,” but that’s far from the only concern.

As one would expect, China has denied any nefarious plot centered on Jensen Huang. A Chinese foreign ministry spokesperson on Monday said, “The narrative about so-called Chinese spies is entirely based on groundless speculation—it’s nothing more than slander and smearing.”

But according to reporting by The New York Times and the Financial Times, US intelligence officials also claim that Alice Huang, also known as Huang Le, was reporting to China’s Ministry of State Security intelligence agency. On Monday, Megaspeed said on LinkedIn that US authorities found “no evidence of any violations concerning the illicit transfer of chips or any illegal activities." (There’s no indication so far that Alice Huang, a one-time Chinese state journalist, is an employee or “officer” of Chinese intelligence; she’s believed to be an “asset,” an outsider who aids an intelligence agency, sometimes for pay, sometimes not.)

China, though, has elevated economic espionage to a core pillar of its economic and military development, stealing the designs for everything from US fighter jets to solar panels and commercial paint recipes. “Honeypots” or “honey traps”—positioning an alluring operative to seduce or develop a relationship with a key target—are one of intelligence’s most infamous and well-established strategies. French officials have warned specifically about Chinese honeypots since at least 2011, when intelligence reports cited a pharmaceutical researcher who was seduced and then blackmailed by a Chinese spy operation.

Over the past quarter of a century, as Silicon Valley emerged as a center of political and economic power, it has become a key focus of Chinese intelligence. In one plot, a Chinese national named Fang Fang spent years trying to cozy up to Bay Area politicians, including then US Representative Eric Swalwell. (While intelligence officials at the time reportedly believed Fang to be an operative, law enforcement officials never brought any charges against her). When traveling to China, US business executives have become accustomed to leaving their regular phones and computers at home and using burner devices to prevent the installation of spyware or malware (as I did, when I traveled to China for WIRED in 2019 to report on Huawei). In 2012, when Leon Panetta was making his first trip to China as defense secretary, he and his team were advised to bathe and shower in the dark in the event his hotel room was bugged. Last year, the threat of honeypots prompted the US government to explicitly ban personnel working in China and Hong Kong from engaging in romantic or sexual relationships with Chinese citizens. More recently, US officials joined with allies to warn about China’s increasingly sophisticated “LinkedIn espionage”: using the business-focused social networking site to recruit and build relationships with unwitting executives.

Alice Huang’s Megaspeed, for its part, is a major Pacific region player in cloud services focused on the advanced computer chips that are the heart of Nvidia’s business. The Biden administration had sharply limited the export of cutting-edge chips globally to slow China’s development of AI, but Jensen Huang has successfully lobbied the Trump administration to loosen those restrictions.

Speaking at a press conference on Monday, Donald Trump denied having prior knowledge of Alice Huang’s potential espionage. “I didn’t hear about it then, but a lot of people have heard about it, and Jensen totally denies that,” Trump said. “I asked him that question: ‘Did you have any idea that she was a spy?’ He said, ‘I had no idea that she was a spy.’” On Tuesday, the Times reported that Marco Rubio had briefed Trump in May, right before Jensen Huang joined the President on Air Force One for a trip to China.

The veracity of Trump's claims will surely be investigated, especially in the context of the famous Watergate maxim: “What did the president know and when did he know it?” Nvidia’s CEO has been publicly linked to the Megaspeed executive since at least 2024 and Alice’s husband, Kang Weigh, has reportedly known Jensen since the 2010s. WIRED’s Lauren Goode reported on Monday that Jensen Huang, starting in 2025, hired Risa Heller’s crisis communications firm, which is well-versed in handling public sex scandals. This spring, secretary of state Marco Rubio evidently helped craft a classified memo inside the administration warning officials about Jensen Huang’s potentially compromising relationship.

Jensen Huang—who has developed an important reputation as AI’s #1 cheerleader at the frontier of AI labs—is now stuck in an awkward situation. Is he an idiot, unaware that his paramour might have been a foreign intelligence asset? Merely reckless with his own reputation, fiduciary responsibility to Nvidia, and the technological security of the United States? Or a witting accomplice to undermining US national security?

Regardless of the truth, Nvidia loses. Shareholders have ridden a roller-coaster that has seen Nvidia’s stock rise by about 600 percent over the past five years, becoming the world’s second-most valuable company. In the past month, the stock has plummeted by a quarter. The disaster for Nvidia is just beginning, it seems, as lawsuits loom, including by its own shareholders.

Similarly, the Trump administration is likely to face real questions from the newly Democratic congress: When were the president and other top officials briefed about this intelligence threat? What communication, if any, did they make to Nvidia and to whom? Should more have been done by US intelligence to block Alice Huang from having access to Nvidia and its CEO?

The fact that Megaspeed is a major customer of Nvidia makes everything more complicated. Indeed, reports have circulated for at least a year that the US was concerned that Megaspeed was diverting chips to Chinese intelligence agencies, an allegation that could have massive consequences for the global AI race. Bloomberg this week reported that Megaspeed's subsidiaries have purchased billions of dollars worth of Nvidia chips and that an audit requested by the US government last year found that Megaspeed couldn’t initially account for some 50,000 chips it had purchased.

Meanwhile, Nvidia’s Huang and President Trump developed a uniquely close professional relationship. They’ve met multiple times as Huang has pushed to loosen Biden-era export controls, which Trump has repeatedly relaxed; Huang has donated $25 million to Trump’s ballroom project; while en route to Beijing earlier this year with a delegation of tech leaders, including Elon Musk and Tim Cook, Trump’s Air Force One actually made a special stop in Alaska to pick up Huang; Huang also attended a White House state dinner for Chinese president Xi Jinping and Trump, who in turn, awarded him a National Medal of Science earlier this fall.

The implication that Trump knew and did nothing for a year would make this one of the greatest counterintelligence failures of the past decade. There will be consequences: Across history, many have gone to prison for honeypot scandals; a former adviser was arrested just last year in a similar plot; though he was acquitted of committing economic espionage, he was convicted for making false statements to investigators.

Even in normal circumstances, corporate America’s tolerance of CEO misbehavior has shrunken dramatically. In recent years, CEOs at companies as varied as Nestlé, Kohl’s, BP, NBCUniversal, and the railroad Norfolk Southern have all resigned or been forced out after inappropriate relationships that violated company standards.

If Nvidia were any other company and Jensen Huang any other CEO, the facts of the scandal currently known would be enough to topple some heads. But Trump is still president, and November is only a third over. It’s not even clear that this will rank as the biggest scandal of the month—but it should.

Square Enix Sees Huge Increase in Digital Game Sales, Upping Its Financial Forecast

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Much like Capcom, Square Enix is seeing a significant surge in digital game sales, as opposed to physical, with the latter now in steady decline.

In fact, Square Enix's latest financial report is pretty eye-opening. It makes for a positive assessment of the company — something of a rarity over the last several years — and it's primarily down to the aforementioned increase in digital purchases.

The publisher reveals that digital sales have practically exploded, jumping from 5.83 million to 8.19 million year-on-year. It cites healthy numbers for Final Fantasy 7 Rebirth's Nintendo Switch 2 and Xbox ports, and The Adventures of Elliot — the action RPG that was released back in the summer.

In total, a whopping 89% of all Square Enix's sales were digital over the last six months, while physical sales dropped from 1.09 million units to 1.01.

So while there's still some demand for boxed games, the slice of the pie is only getting smaller.

All of this has led to the Japanese company upping its financial forecast for the rest of the fiscal year, going from ¥298,000 million to ¥310,000 million. While not a massive jump, it's still a good sign, given the publisher's recent struggles.

Of course, all eyes will now be on Final Fantasy 7 Revelation, which launches on the 8th April. It'll be the first game in the trilogy to release on multiple platforms — a move that Square Enix thinks could make a big difference after Rebirth couldn't come close to matching Remake's initial sales.

Shrek 2 Remastered Has No Official Reviews Yet, But The User Reviews Are Quite Bizarre

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It has a high score on Xbox right now, but a Mixed one on Steam

Yesterday marked the launch of Shrek 2 Remastered on Xbox Series X and Xbox Series S (along with other platforms), and if you're wondering why nobody seems to be talking about it, it's because there are no official reviews yet.

It seems codes were only sent out at launch for this one, which means it's going to take a while for the critics to catch up and share their thoughts on this remaster of an original Xbox cult classic.

We can at least see what the initial user reviews are saying about it though, and if you look at the Xbox Store, everything seems to be pretty rosy. Shrek 2 Remastered has a 4.3/5 rating on the US Xbox Store as things stand, with 76% of people giving it a 5-star review, while most of the (minimal) grumbles are focused on bugs and crashes.

The Steam reviews for Shrek 2 Remastered are quite bizarre though. Over there, the game currently has a "Mixed" average rating based on over 350 user reviews, with 46% of people rating it "Positive" overall.

If you look at the "Most Helpful" reviews that appear first, there are multiple people suggesting it's not a great port, highlighting issues with bugged cutscenes, a lack of settings options, and even claims that the CPUs are less intelligent than they were in the original game (although others debate that it plays "exactly the same" as it used to!). There are also multiple accusations that the remaster might have been partially put together using generative AI, although this is just speculation and not confirmation by any means.

Some people are totally put off by all these issues, while others are still really enjoying their time with the game.

Satan Claus on Steam: "Shrek 2 was one of my favourite games growing up, and I honestly can't explain how happy I am to have the chance to play it again after all these years. It's just as special as I remember, and seeing it brought back with changes that do the original justice is wonderful. Even better, being able to enjoy it on modern hardware, especially the Steam Deck, feels like a dream come true. Some childhood memories are worth revisiting, and this is absolutely one of them."

Mikey on Steam: "I was really hyped for this remaster since this game is a game I grew up playing with my sister, but I am sort of disappointed with the state it is in right now. Lots of camera bugs, cutscenes ending aprubtly, missing skybox textures, characters looking woody and uncanny and lots more. It's like they just threw a paint bucket on it and called it a day. I'll wait for patches, since there still is hope."

As mentioned, Shrek 2 Remastered is getting a much better reception on Xbox than Steam right now, so perhaps we ended up getting the better version (or Xbox fans are more lenient), and you can check it out for yourself at the price of £22.49 / $26.99 for Series X and Series S if you're interested.

We do actually have a review copy of the game that we'll hopefully be able to check out soon, so stay tuned for our impressions in the near future. We'll also keep an eye out for raw Xbox Series X and Xbox Series S footage comparisons so you can get an idea of how it plays across both consoles.

For now though, we'll leave you with some basic gameplay from Shrek 2 Remastered down below.

Capline Dental Services Reports 60% Faster Dental Credentialing Through AI-Driven Automated Workflows

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Houston-based dental revenue cycle management company says automated profile checks, payer follow-up, and status tracking are helping providers reach active payer status sooner.

HOUSTON, Nov. 11, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Capline Dental Services (CDS), a U.S.-based dental credentialing company, has reported a 60% acceleration in dental credentialing after introducing AI-driven automated workflows across its provider enrollment process. The workflows cover provider profile validation, application preparation, payer follow-up, and status tracking, the areas where credentialing files commonly lose time.

Credentialing is the key step that decides when a dentist can bill a payer as an in-network provider. Until an application is approved and the provider is active, claims may be held, paid at out-of-network rates, or denied. Timelines vary by payer, but according to CDS, most fall between 60 and 180 days, with 90 to 120 days being common. For a practice adding an associate or opening a new practice, each week of waiting is a week in which that provider's schedule cannot produce fully insured revenue.

"Credentialing rarely stalls because of one large error," said Abhinav Rastogi, Founder of Capline Dental Services. "It stalls when a document is missing, details do not match across forms, or a payer request sits unanswered. Automation helps us catch those gaps early and keep every file moving, while our credentialing specialists remain accountable for each application."

The announcement comes as administrative work continues to weigh on dental practices. The American Dental Association's Health Policy Institute reports that 72.5% of U.S. dentists owned private practices in 2023, down from 84.7% in 2005. For practices that add associates or open new locations, every new provider means another round of payer enrollment, and the provider pool is large: the ADA counts 205,088 professionally active dentists in the United States as of 2025.

The credentialing workload carries a measurable cost of its own. In a 2019 survey of 1,240 physician practices, CAQH found that practices exchanging credentialing information through multiple methods spent an average of $2,068 a month on the work, compared with $1,249.86 for practices using a single platform. The average practice in that survey held 20.2 health plan contracts, each with its own verification standards. Dental offices enrolling providers with several payers face a similar patchwork of requirements.

Delays seldom trace back to a single mistake. They build from smaller gaps: an incomplete document, a detail that differs between the application and the provider's CAQH profile, or a payer request that goes unanswered for days. Because each payer has its own forms and review rules, these gaps multiply when a practice applies to several networks at once. Front desk and office teams often end up chasing status updates between patient calls, making consistent follow-up hard to maintain.

The documentation side is simple but detail-sensitive. Practices typically need license and education details, malpractice coverage, and provider credentials ready before an application can move. CDS sends a checklist at the start so nothing is overlooked, and its automated checks review the file for gaps before it goes to the payer.

AI supports the credentialing file from the first document a provider uploads to the last payer follow-up, with a person reviewing the work before anything reaches a payer. The idea is simple: catch errors before the payer does.

CDS says its AI-enabled systems help its team analyze patterns, flag documentation gaps, and prioritize follow-ups, while human oversight stays in place on every account. In credentialing, that means software handles the auditing, verification, form filling, and reminders, while a team member reviews every application before submission and handles payer communication when a file needs attention.

The same approach applies when practices grow. When an associate or specialist joins, CDS streamlines the enrollment steps to reduce provider downtime and keep schedules productive.

Keeping Providers Credentialed After Approval

Approval is not the end of the work. CAQH asks providers to re-attest their profile data every 120 days, and recredentialing comes around again later. CDS manages CAQH profile creation, document uploads, updates, and attestations, along with recredentialing, EFT enrollment, Medicare enrollment, and, where payers allow it, fee schedule support. The goal is for providers to stay active and for payments to flow correctly once enrollment is complete.

Connecting Credentialing to Dental Revenue Cycle Management

CDS treats credentialing as one part of dental revenue cycle management rather than a stand-alone task. An enrollment error that goes unnoticed can surface months later as a rejected claim, which is why the company connects credentialing with dental billing and collection, and dental eligibility verification. Keeping these functions under one team gives practices a clearer view of where payments are getting delayed.

"Faster credentialing only helps if the enrollment is set up correctly," Abhinav Rastogi added. "Our aim is for a provider to reach active status with a clean setup, so claims process properly from the first insured visit."

CDS has served more than 1.4K+ clients and has over 12 years of experience supporting general dentists, specialists, and multi-location practices. Its team handles dental billing, credentialing, insurance verification, and patient statements through HIPAA-guided workflows. Practices that want to understand how CDS manages provider enrollment can request a free consultation through the company's website. CDS also offers a free dental revenue health check that reviews denial trends, payer performance, A/R days, and eligibility gaps.

Capline Dental Services is a Houston-based dental revenue cycle management company that supports dental practices across the United States. With more than 12 years of experience, it works with general dentists, specialists, and multi-location practices on dental billing and collection, dental credentialing, dental eligibility verification, dental insurance verification, and dental patient statements. Its workflows follow HIPAA guidelines to support secure data handling.

To learn more about Capline's dental revenue cycle services, visit https://www.caplinedentalservices.com/

Tottenham Hotspur star Pedro Porro is a target for both Real Madrid and Barcelona - and appears to be heading back to Spain

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Tottenham Hotspur full-back Pedro Porro has opened up about leaving N17 once again.

The World Cup winner signed a new deal with the Lilywhites over the summer to keep him in North London until 2031, after back-to-back 17th-place finishes in the Premier League.

After well-documented interest from Spanish giants, the Tottenham man has opened up to boldly tell reporters “it's true” he will return to his homeland, as Real Madrid and Barcelona circle for his signature.

In comments relayed by Sport Witness, Porro has opened up once again to link himself away from Spurs.

The outlet records this as the sixth time that the Spaniard has spoken about moving – despite the implication that he's talking about his long-term future – with Porro even prefacing his comments this time with, “I don’t want to get into any trouble”.

“I’ve just renewed my contract with Tottenham,” Porro began, “I have a great deal of respect for my club, and I’m very grateful for the support they give me.

“It’s true, and no secret, that I hope to return to Spain one day, as it’s my home country. But first and foremost, I want to emphasise my respect for the club I’m currently under contract with. As for the future, well, we’ll see.”

Whether Porro really is talking about returning to Spain now or in the distant future will surely become apparent, should Barça come calling for his services.

TIM S.A. (TIMB) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bad debt rose sharply, but B2B and broadband momentum kept revenue growing.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

TIM S.A. (TIMB +5.52%) reported growth in service revenue and EBITDA for the third quarter of 2026, driven by expansion in broadband and B2B segments alongside disciplined cost management. Management reported that artificial intelligence is improving operational efficiency, specifically in customer care and network maintenance. The company maintained a cash position of BRL 5.2 billion while reducing gross debt and confirming its full-year guidance for 2026 shareholder remuneration. Strategic priorities for 2027 include accelerating mobile operational KPIs and pursuing selective M&A opportunities in the broadband and B2B sectors.

Operator: Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. Welcome to TIM SA 2026 Third Quarter Results Video Conference Call. We would like to inform you that this event is being recorded. [Operator Instructions] [Presentation]

Operator: Hello, everyone, and welcome to Tim's Third Quarter 2026 Results Presentation. Following today's presentation, Alberto Griselli, CEO; Andrea Viegas, CFO; and the Investor Relations team will be available for the live Q&A session. Before we begin, please note that this presentation may contain forward-looking statements, which are subject to risks and uncertainties. And now I'll hand it over to Alberto.

Alberto Griselli: Hello everyone. Our third quarter results reinforce the consistent trajectory we have followed throughout 2026. During the first nine months of the year, we continued to grow revenue, improve profitability, and convert our operating performance into cash. This reflects the discipline with which we are executing our strategy while maintaining a solid financial position. Service revenue increased by 5.8%, normalized EBITDA grew by 6.8%, and operating cash flow increased by 11.6%. These results keep us on track to deliver our 2026 guidance. Looking specifically at the third quarter, service revenues reached approximately BRL 6.9 billion, an increase of 5.3% compared with the same period last year. In B2C, we continue to evolve our portfolio across mobile and broadband.

We are developing propositions for different customer needs, creating more value for our clients and strengthening our relationship with them over time. Within mobile, revenue grew 4% year over year, supported by ARPU expansion. Postpaid continued a positive trajectory, while prepaid showed the first signs of recovery with higher-value recharges and an improved revenue trend despite a more challenging competitive environment. In broadband, Ultra Fibra revenue increased by 12.7% year over year, while the customer base recorded double-digit growth for the third consecutive quarter. In B2B, revenue grew by 19.3% over the last 12 months, increasing their contribution to our revenue mix. We continue to broaden our portfolio through new partnerships and contracts in established verticals and connected vehicles.

For example, our partnership with BYD strengthens our position in the automotive segment, while in Smart Cities, the SmartView project expands our proposition beyond smart lighting into 5G-enabled public safety. Think Cloud AI by V8 also adds cloud, data, and artificial intelligence capabilities to our offering. Together, these developments reflect the continued evolution of our B2B business beyond traditional connectivity. We are also making practical progress in the use of artificial intelligence in customer care. AI agents already support billing and collection journeys, with our billing agent reducing average handling time by 86% compared to human interactions. We are now expanding their use to offers and sales.

In our network, predictive maintenance has helped prevent, on average, 93% of failures at the sites identified by the model. Turning to our financial performance, revenue continued to grow faster than costs, with OpEx up 3.9% in the quarter. This supported normalized EBITDA expansion of nearly 7%, taking the margin above 52% on an after IFRS 16 basis. The result was even stronger: EBITDA grew close to 8%, while margin expanded by around 1 percentage point to 41.3%. Also benefiting from costs below inflation, our operating performance continued to translate into cash generation. In the first nine months of 2026, operating cash flow reached almost BRL 5 billion, an increase of 11.6% year over year.

The operating cash flow margin reached 23.9%, improving by 1.3 percentage points. Cash generation remains an important part of our performance. It supports our ability to invest in the business while preserving financial flexibility. We ended the quarter with 5.2 billion in cash. Even after the full payment of VA and I-Systems, gross debt declined by 3.7% compared with the previous year and leverage remained at a lower level. This indicator shows that our operating performance continues to translate into a healthy balance sheet, solid liquidity, and controlled leverage. As we move into the final part of the year, our priorities remain consistent. In B2C, we will continue to evolve our value proposition across mobile and broadband.

In B2B, we will continue to expand our portfolio and develop our presence in established verticals. At the same time, we are renewing the Instituto TIM with a focus on technology-enabled education, dignity, and gender equity, contributing to a more inclusive and digital society. Our results reflect a business that continues to evolve, improve profitability, and generate cash. We will continue to execute our strategy with consistency and discipline. Thank you. And now let's move to the live Q&A.

Alberto Griselli: So thank you all for joining our call today. As the market continues to evolve, we are driving our strategy to accelerate mobile operational KPIs and enter 2027 at a stronger pace. As we said, at the same time, we continue to expand opportunities in broadband and B2B. I would like to thank again our entire team for the commitment and focus to deliver on our strategy, and we are all looking forward to meeting you in the coming days.

Flash memory maker YMTC is pushing ahead with plans for a Shanghai IPO as soon as early 2027

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

The Chinese NAND flash maker plans to raise 33 billion yuan ($4.9 billion) in the offering, according to Bloomberg

Yangtze Memory Technologies Co., China's biggest maker of NAND flash memory, is aiming to go public on the Shanghai Stock Exchange as soon as the first quarter of 2027, according to Bloomberg.

The Wuhan-based company, known as YMTC, plans to list on the exchange's tech-focused Star board, and that effort remains in progress, according to Bloomberg. YMTC advanced to the following phase in September, with plans to raise 33 billion yuan ($4.9 billion). The Shanghai Stock Exchange put its initial set of questions to the company in early September, which marked the formal start of the review.

The IPO had been paused as of last week, according to Reuters, which based its report on an internal company memo and on sources with knowledge of the situation.

China's stocks regulator held a meeting with investment banks, where it informally told them to halt IPOs, according to the Financial Times, which cited three unidentified people. Big stake sales by existing shareholders are also on temporary hold until markets rebound.

The pause is not a “one-size-fits-all” measure, according to Bloomberg, which cited a Cailian report quoting a senior investment banker. The banker said that easing the flow of new shares a bit suits what the market currently needs and should not be taken as IPOs being shut down entirely.

Dealmaking has slowed after a wave of fundraising that ran through the end of summer, according to Bloomberg. That wave included CXMT’s 66.6 billion yuan ($9.9 billion) offering in July. YMTC's progress may calm worries over swings in the market and the prospect that Beijing will delay listings for several of its biggest companies.

YMTC filed for a Shanghai IPO in August to raise $4.9 billion. Its prospectus outlined plans to issue new stock equal to 10% to 12% of its post-offering share capital, with an overallotment option of up to 15%. The company said it would use the proceeds to upgrade production lines and develop next-generation storage products, and it said it expected to list in 2027.

YMTC ranks third among NAND memory makers by shipments, behind Samsung Electronics Co. and SK Hynix, according to Bloomberg, which cited Counterpoint Research data. YMTC passed Kioxia Holdings and Micron $MU Technology to reach that position.

'The possibilities are truly breathtaking': NASA's Roman Space Telescope will take better images and last longer than experts predicted

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

NASA's Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope could capture images 30% sharper than — and remain operational for twice as long as — initially expected. Researchers say these "remarkable" developments could greatly enhance the volume and quality of the data they will analyze.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

NASA's Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope is nearing its final destination more than 1 million miles (1.6 million kilometers) from Earth, where it will soon scour the universe for a host of cosmological treasures, including an estimated 100,000 new alien worlds.

But even before its mission truly begins, recent developments suggest the state-of-the-art spacecraft could take better images than — and remain operational for more than twice as long as — initially expected, further delighting already-excited astronomers.

"It is fantastic to learn that Roman is not just reaching its design goals but exceeding them," Eamonn Kerins, an astronomer at the University of Manchester in the U.K. who will help lead one of Roman's major surveys, told Live Science.

Roman is NASA's latest flagship space telescope, following in the footsteps of iconic observatories like the Hubble Space Telescope and the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST). The $4 billion machine ‪—‬ which is over 42 feet (12.7 meters) long and weighs around the same as a fully grown Tyrannosaurus rex ‪—‬ successfully launched into space on Aug. 30, more than a decade after scientists started building it.

The telescope is now en route to Lagrange point 2 (L2), a fixed point relative to our planet where Earth's gravity and the sun's gravity cancel each other out. There, Roman will join several other important spacecraft, including JWST and the European Space Agency's Euclid telescope.

Roman was launched into space on board one of SpaceX's Falcon heavy rockets. It lifted off Aug. 30 from NASA's Kennedy Space Center in Florida.

During Roman's journey to L2, which is currently around 85% complete, mission scientists have remotely switched on the telescope's various instruments to test whether they function as expected. So far, this "commissioning process" has gone off without a hitch, and by the time Roman arrives in less than two weeks, it should be ready to snap its first in-depth images of the cosmos.

Researchers were already pumped to see what Roman will be able to do once it is in place and fully switched on. However, that excitement has further intensified in recent weeks, thanks to two postlaunch developments.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

First, a series of near-perfect rocket burns, executed during and shortly after launch, saved more than a decade's worth of fuel that can now be used to extend Roman's mission. Second, after remotely adjusting the telescope's mirror, researchers discovered it should be capable of snapping images with around 30% greater detail than engineers predicted.

The team that designed, launched and remotely commissioned the orbital observatory has "done an amazing job" and "their work has ensured that Roman is more than up to the task," Kerins said.

Shortly after Roman arrived in space on board a SpaceX Falcon Heavy rocket that lifted off from NASA's Kennedy Space Center in Florida, mission scientists conducted two major burns, or engine firings, which gave the spacecraft enough momentum to escape Earth's orbit and make the journey to L2.

Roman is around the same size as a small truck and was packed into the nose cone of the Falcon Heavy rocket prior to launch.

Thanks to a combination of "exquisite planning by our orbital dynamics team" and "brilliant execution by the operations team," these burns were so precise that each one saved up to four years' worth of fuel, Jamie Dunn, director of NASA's Goddard Space Flight Center in Greenbelt, Maryland, said in a Sept. 14 statement.

On top of this, Roman ended up being much lighter than initially planned, which allowed engineers to pack another four years' worth of fuel into the spacecraft before launch. "Since Roman's [weight] was lower than we budgeted for, we were able to fill the propellant tanks to their capacity rather than only filling them as much as we needed," Alison Rao, the Roman propulsion lead at NASA Goddard, said in the statement.

In total, these savings could give Roman an additional 12 years of fuel, which could more than double the mission's planned 10-year lifespan. That means Roman could still be capturing images of the cosmos in 2048.

Given that Roman can collect data up to 1,000 times faster than Hubble, this extended lifespan will greatly enhance the amount of data the new space telescope will be able to capture. "With a 20-year Roman lifetime, we could, in theory, do over 20,000 years of Hubble survey work," Kerins said. "That's a lot of science!"

Roman is now on its way to L2, where the gravity of Earth and the sun cancel out, allowing it to remain in one place without expending much fuel.

This prolonged lifespan will let astronomers compare images of specific objects decades apart — such as expanding nebulas or colliding galaxies — allowing them to detect changes they otherwise might have missed.

It may also let them study objects or phenomena that we haven't even discovered yet, which is particularly exciting, Kerins noted. "The real show-stopper results will be new things we just aren't even thinking about right now," he said. "The possibilities are truly breathtaking."

There is also the chance that Roman's mission could be extended even further, because engineers often find clever ways of making active spacecraft last longer. For example, Hubble is more than two decades past its initial mission parameters, and the recently deceased Swift Observatory lasted 10 times longer than initially predicted before falling back to Earth last week.

Roman's secret weapon is arguably its Wide Field Instrument (WFI), made up of a 288-megapixel camera attached to a 7.9-foot (2.4 m) mirror, which will capture ultra-detailed images with a field of view roughly 100 times larger than Hubble's.

Roman's WFI consists of a 288-megapixel camera attached to a 7.9-foot (2.4 m) mirror. It will be capable of capturing images with a field of view 100 times greater than Hubble.

We have already seen the first WFI images — a series of blurry, green dots captured during a test of the instrument's 18 different light detectors ( see below). However, those photos were nothing like what WFI will capture in the future; those images will be so detailed that you would need dozens of high-definition TVs to properly display a single snap in full resolution, Kerins said.

After Roman completes the initial WFI commissioning phase, those images will be around 30% sharper than designers predicted. "All of the images from Roman's cosmic surveys will be even sharper than we expected," Alden Jurling, a senior optical engineer who works on Roman at NASA Goddard, said in an Oct. 14 statement. "It's a great outcome."

The improvements were first noticed after the WFI's car-sized mirror was realigned by just one-sixteenth the diameter of a human hair, demonstrating just how intricate the commissioning process has been. "Calibrating an instrument is like tuning a piano or guitar before a concert," Maxime Rizzo, a WFI systems engineer at NASA Goddard, said in the statement. "You adjust everything until it's just how you want it and then leave the settings alone so it performs consistently while you use it."

NASA released Roman's first photos on Sept. 15. Each small square represents one of the telescope's light detectors, and each blurry green spot is a distant star. (This is just a test image and does not represent how the telescope's proper photos will look.)

The improved image quality could really come to the fore during Roman's hunt for potentially habitable exoplanets, which is what Live Science's readers are most excited about for this mission.

Kerins, who is leading the Exoplanet Demographics Working Group for the Transits in the Roman Exoplanets Survey, is particularly excited about this development, but he said it does not necessarily guarantee that Roman will find more exoplanets than initially predicted.

The rest of Roman's commissioning phase is also proceeding as planned.

Roman's coronagraph instrument, which will block out the light from distant stars to allow WFI to spot alien worlds, has passed its main commissioning phase and is working as intended, according to a NASA blog post.

Roman has completed around 85% of its journey to L2 and should arrive at its final destination in the next few weeks. (The timeline in this image is a screenshot from Roman's commissioning process website, captured on Nov. 11.)

Meanwhile, a global network of ground stations, which will receive up to 1.4 terabytes of data from Roman every day, has established contact with the spacecraft and is receiving signals as expected, another blog post revealed.

All of these early wins raise hopes that Roman can have a long and successful career, and are a testament to the years of work that went into designing the spacecraft and planning the mission.

"What we're seeing now with the actual optical performance and estimated lifetime of Roman is just how good that design process has been," Kerins said. "It's a remarkable achievement. I can't wait to see what Roman is going to give us."

Harry is a U.K.-based senior staff writer at Live Science. He studied marine biology at the University of Exeter before training to become a journalist. He covers a wide range of topics including space exploration, planetary science, space weather, climate change, animal behavior and paleontology. His recent work on the solar maximum won "best space submission" at the 2024 Aerospace Media Awards and was shortlisted in the "top scoop" category at the NCTJ Awards for Excellence in 2023. He also writes Live Science's weekly Earth from space series.

Se potesse rivivere un’esperienza della sua carriera a Roma, quale sceglierebbe? "Oltre ai trofei, ricordi indelebili, ripenso al legame unico che si era creato nello spogliatoio nell’anno dello scudetto. Con Capello in panchina ho dato il meglio di me, perché c’era un legame speciale: ero un po’ il suo figlioccio".

Alla domanda se fosse orgoglioso del proprio percorso di vita, ha risposto che il sé bambino sarebbe fiero dell'uomo che è diventato. Ha raccontato la sua infanzia complessa, in cui il calcio era un'ancora di salvezza e una speranza di riscatto, menzionando con un pizzico di ironia la bocciatura ricevuta in prima elementare a sette anni.
